Hesitation/stutter

Chapter 26: When the Ostrich Plan Fails

[T/n note on title]1

Ji Yuan did not cover his entire head, but left a gap, through which he could see tufts of hair seeping through the cracks in the door to the room, gathering more and more inside…

His heart was cold, his body was sweating, and the brand-new bedding was soaked with Ji Yuan’s sweat and had become damp.

Ji Yuan didn’t dare to move. He rapidly thought of countermeasures in his mind. Several times, he wanted to rush out and escape.

“Hooo…”

An inhuman voice sounded in the room, and Ji Yuan, who was already scared to death, froze.

Through the gap in the covers, he watched the pile of hair slowly rise, and a pitch-black outline of a human figure appeared. Although everything else around it in his vision was blurry, he could see it very clearly, even if he really would rather not have.

The bone-chilling chill continued to spread. Even covered in the quilt, he couldn’t feel any warmth at all.

‘What do I do? What do I do? This is completely different from a regular ghost! If it’s not a regular ghost, what kind of ghost even is it?’

Ji Yuan clung tightly to the end of the quilt. His intense fear and rapid heartbeat made his entire body tremble uncontrollably.

This time, there were no traveling merchants, nor young heroes, and his opponent was not someone whom he could communicate with like Lu Shanjun.

A strong yin energy and deadly intent permeated the entire Ju’an Pavilion.

“Kkkkk… Kkkkk…”

The otherworldly and eerie sound of bone rubbing together grew closer and closer, so close that it seemed to be separated from him by only a thin layer of quilt.

He could feel the malice that wanted to kill, the greed and desire for life. Hiding under the quilt, Ji Yuan’s pupils had shrunk to the size of needles.

I will die! I can’t escape!

This was not like watching horror movies or Hong Kong horror films2 through a screen. This sense of fear and despair was suffocating and made him feel powerless.

Suddenly, Ji Yuan discovered that this feeling of powerlessness was actually not caused by any physical hormones in his body, but that, in fact, wisps of white energy were leaving his body.

‘It’s sucking my yang energy!’

“Hooo…”

He felt a huge pressure on his body, which gradually made him unable to move, and soon even breathing became difficult.

If Ji Yuan weren’t covered by a quilt, he might have been able to see a filthy shadowy figure reaching out with pale, twisted limbs that were somehow attached to his main body…

The terrifying hissing and the uncontrolled state of his body reminded Ji Yuan of the time when he first traveled to this world.

At this moment, Ji Yuan felt an inexplicable surge of anger.

‘I overcame the critical dead end in front of the tiger spirit in that mountain temple, but now I’m going to die here for no reason? I’m not freaking willing to give in! Not at all!’

He grit his teeth, pale eyes bloodshot, eyelids shaking violently, trying to stretch out the fingers of his right hand.

“I just came to this world, I just discovered my own powers, I still have a lot of things to do, and I still have a lot of things I want to do. I still want to see the magic in this world!’

Regardless of whether he thought it would work, Ji Yuan kept visualizing the Lanke’s chess game, imagining the piece. It was the only method he could think of at that moment.

Ji Yuan’s heart grew fierce, and he opened his eyes completely, regardless of the pain. With a breath, he shook slightly, breaking out of his trance, and regained control in an instant.

‘I’m-!’

“-not willing!!!”

The words roared out of his mouth. Ji Yuan abruptly stood up at the same movement, lifted the quilt, and formed his arms and hands into sword fingers. He stabbed fiercely behind the quilt in a desperate struggle. A mysterious energy was flowing down his arm, materializing an illusory chess piece at his fingertips.

“Get out of here!!!”

Hum…

Ji Yuan’s arm seemed to be filled with a faint white light, and in the next moment, the chess piece and fingertips touched the ghost.

“Aaaaaahhhhhh-“

As the sword fingers and chess piece penetrated the ghost’s soul, a terrible scream sounded, so sharp it caused extreme pain to Ji Yuan’s eardrums.

Woo woo woo…

The gusts of yin wind seemed to swirl, and the foul and terrifying yin qi swirled around Ji Yuan’s right arm, like clothes in a drum washing machine.

Ji Yuan felt as if his entire right hand had been entirely frozen. The bone-piercing cold was like steel needles constantly piercing into the flesh of his right arm, and the tingling and chill were soon difficult to endure any longer.

In the very next moment…

A “bang!” sounded.

The black shadow bas blasted back and hit the door, directly passing through it, and quickly escaped back into the well in the courtyard.

Ji Yuan, on the other hand, stretched out his right arm and widened his eyes. He held this posture for about ten seconds, then stood on the bed and swayed twice. His body went limp and fell backwards.

With a “pop,” Ji Yuan fell back on the bed and passed out.

This shrill scream was so eerie and penetrated the night air. No one could tell just how many residents in that corner of Tian Niu Square were suddenly awakened by this unidentifiable scream in the middle of the night, hiding under their quilts and not daring to move.

Even some households whose lights were still on hurriedly blew out the candles, for fear of attracting something evil.

At this moment, on Temple Administration Lane,3 in the northwest of Ning’an County, where the Ning’an County’s City God Temple was located…

The golden body4 in the temple vibrated.

Beneath the surface of what ordinary people could perceive, the City God of Ning’an County was already standing in the great hall.

“Where is Ye Xunyou? Go to the Soul Locking Well of Tian Niu Square in the south of the city to investigate!”

“I have received your orders!”

Two Yin emissaries5 appeared, clothed in black from head to toe. One carried a hook sword,6 and the other had a saber hanging from his waist. They turned into two shadows, rushed out of the City God Temple, and headed towards Tian Niu Square.

. . .

In Ju’an Pavilion, Ji Yuan rubbed his head and sat up from the bed. When he passed out, he had hit the back of his head against the edge of the bed. Fortunately, his head was pretty hard, otherwise he would have had a concussion.

Ji Yuan still carried the impression of that last moment. With one finger, he had knocked the ghost-like thing flying, and it had then escaped. Judging from its reaction, it hadn’t just left unscathed, and was probably quite frightened.

After the whole mess just now, Ji Yuan felt much more courageous, so he put on his coat and got out of the bed.

His right arm was still cold and numb, and his whole body was a little weak, but it was basically nothing serious.

He pulled open the wooden latch, and the doors of the main room opened with a creak.

The stars shone brightly above. The branches of the jujube trees were swaying, and the well under the shade was completely shrouded in darkness.

Whooo… Whoooo…

He didn’t know whether it was simply a cool breeze or a gust of wind, but Ji Yuan to shivered a little.

He stood at the door of the main room feeling restless. The expression on his face was cloudy and uncertain. He considered several times whether he should simply run away now. After all, he might not be so lucky next time.

“There are mortals living in the Ju’an Pavilion again?”

Suddenly, a slightly surprised voice floated in from outside the courtyard, and two figures in black uniforms, armed with weapons, passed directly through the main gate and entered the courtyard.

Ji Yuan’s heart trembled and his pupils shrank, realizing that he could actually see them clearly.

‘Damn it, again! ’

The two figures in black robes were obviously all focusing on the well in the courtyard. They merely gave Ji Yuan a glance as he stood at the door to the main room, and ignored him.

“Strange, most of the hostile energy that overflowed from from the Soul Locking Well has dissipated. What happened here?”

“Lord City God must have noticed something strange!”

“The feng shui pattern of the small pavilion in Ju’an has not been broken, so the evil spirit should not be able to escape. I can feel that it is still in the well!”

“Yes, and its breath is unstable!”

“If that’s the case, it’s a God-given opportunity to kill this creature. We must report back to the Lord City God immediately!”

After the two Yin agents’ brief exchange, the one with the long-handled hook sword stayed by the well, while the one with the saber turned into a vague human-shaped smoke form and drifted away through the door.

Ji Yuan, at the threshold to the main room, couldn’t help but breathe a sigh of relief. It was good that he hadn’t run away just now.

‘They think I can’t see them? Evil spirit? Feng Shui pattern? Lord City God? ’

As a young man who had extensive access to the explosion of resources on the Internet in the 21st century, a few simple words allowed Ji Yuan to infer a lot of things.

There might have been some powerful ghost locked in the well in the courtyard of Ju’an Pavilion. The City God of Ning’an County was not just a clay sculpture in the temple. The two who came here may be the Yin emissaries serving under the jurisdiction of the City God…

He had seen monsters and ghosts before, and now he had witnessed an evil suppressed under the county’s City God.

Ji Yuan’s thoughts turned sharply.

‘Then, does this world have mountain and river gods, or deities, or Buddhas? Should I run now? 36 taels… Is it because of my fingers and powers that the thing in the well was injured?’

In this light, Ji Yuan felt that he had enough confidence to stay and be an audience.

And, deep down, Ji Yuan still had the hope to live out his middle-school fantasies.7 Not everyone had the opportunity to see things like the real City God from the temple.

Ostrich plan, i.e. sticking your head in the sand to ignore something and hoping it goes away

T/n: Apparently Hong Kong has produced a lot of horror films. There are political/cultural reasons for this – Hong Kong has a long history under various colonial powers (I highly encourage you to look it up), the result of which was separate governing and economic systems for some time. This also meant that they weren’t as subject to the rest of China’s regulations on what could and couldn’t be produced in media, such as ghost movies.

庙 (miào): temple/shrine/imperial, 司 (sī): to manage/department, 坊 (fāng) lane/memorial archway, basically the name of the neighborhood. I considered leaving it as Miao Si Lane, as I did with Tian Niu Square, but Tian Niu Square doesn’t directly describe the function of the square itself (it’s just a pretty name for the most part), while this name does (also MTL helpfully translated it as “temple of the temple,” thanks MTL)

Golden statue of the city god in the main shrine. I’m leaving it as “body” and not making it “statue” because this novel has a fun bit of wordplay in conflating various statues as incarnations of the gods themselves, where they can manifest themselves through them – exactly like an extra body.

T/n: Interesting translation here: half of the MTL translated it as “eunuch,” likely referring to the feminine associations of yin energy, while some others used “yin agents.” Considering eunuchs’ positions as administrative aids to high ranking palaces, the first could make sense, but traditionally eunuchs didn’t take up fighting roles (as they do in future chapters) so I left it as “Yin emissary/agent,” where “Yin” refers to their relation to the underworld.

Lit: “long-handled hook”

Think “8th grade syndrome” or “chuunibyou”

Chapter 27: Big Battle

Ji Yuan was still a little hesitant about whether or not he should approach the underworld agent on the opposite side of the courtyard. With his previous concept act, the character he played could be regarded as a one of the immortals, Buddhas, or spirits, right?

However, the Yin emissary in the courtyard was, in fact, also watching Ji Yuan in that moment.

Previously, he had overlooked the mortal because the situation that they had discovered was more important. Now, in retrospect, it seemed to be something wrong with the new resident of Ju’an Pavilion.

After all, in the dead of night, in such a house, this person was just standing at the door to the main rooms and looking out at the yard. He couldn’t possibly be enjoying the cool air in this early spring, right?

‘He might be someone with a keen intuition?’

It’s wasn’t like the Yin emissary had never encountered such people before. Even if he wasn’t one of them, the yin qi in Ju’an Pavilion was so frighteningly strong after the third night watch that even a normal person would have trouble sleeping and frequent nightmares.

After much deliberation, Ji Yuan finally decided to stay away for the time being. If he wanted to pay homage to the City God, he could go and offer incense during the day.1

So, Ji Yuan walked to the front of the house under the eaves as naturally as possible. He looked up at the night sky and sighed, speaking half-truth, half-lie.

“Such a clean starry sky… I haven’t seen it for a long time…”

There weren’t very many things he could see clearly. He hadn’t noticed it on Niu Kui Mountain, but now he realized that the stars in the sky were part of that short list.

There was no cloud cover and no smog pollution. The sky was full of stars and the galaxy above was shining brightly. It was really beautiful!

Now that he knew there was a Yin emissary here, coupled with the lingering courage from his sword fingers from before, Ji Yuan simply sat down on a small stool next to the house, looked at the sky and the yard, and sighed from time to time. He behaved just like an ordinary insomniac.

Of course, most of Ji Yuan’s attention was still focused on the movements of the people in the yard. During this period of meditation, he also tried to visualize the chess pieces, but maybe his body was too weak at the moment, and his spirit was still shaking a little. However, the feeling was still there. This also lent Ji Yuan more confidence in continuing to sit there.

Are you kidding me? I just pointed and that thing ran away, and didn’t I hear the emissary say that the thing’s vitality is badly damaged? Plus, the Yin emissary is still around, so why not be a little tougher?

After waiting for about a quarter of an hour, Ji Yuan felt something different.

A strange smell was gradually thickening. Trying to describe it, it was a bit like the sandalwood fragrance that Ji Yuan’s Grandpa liked to use in his study.

Then, Ji Yuan suddenly realized that it was the scent of the sandalwood in the temple.

His heartbeat accelerated again. It seemed that someone special was coming. Could it be the Ning’an County City God himself?

As the scent of sandalwood gradually approached, Jiy Yuan also sat upright. Unlike other ghosts, he could hear bursts of special footsteps that seemed to have some kind of rhythm, and from what he could tell, there was more than one person.

Wooo… Woooo…

A thin wind seemed to carry the scent of sandalwood that hung in the courtyard. One after another, figures stepped through the gate of Ju’an Pavilion and entered the courtyard. In addition to a couple more sinister-looking figures dressed in black, Ji Yuan could also see four people who at first glance were clearly not simple. They looked like officials, with the robes draped on their bodies, each of a different color.

“Gulp…”

Ji Yuan couldn’t help but swallow quietly. This battle seemed a bit big.

“Greetings to Lord Wu Pan, Lord of Martial Judgement; Lord Jiang Shan, the Lord of Rewards; Fa E, the Lord of Punishment; and Jiu Cha, the Lord of Maintaining Order!”2

The Yin emissaries in the courtyard respectfully saluted the four tall, imposing figures.

These four were obviously not City Gods of Ning’an County, but they definitely received incense offerings and worship of the common people. At least, they seemed to have clay sculptures in the Temple of the City God. They certainly were not ordinary people, otherwise they wouldn’t have brought the scent of sandalwood with them.

Ji Yuan didn’t know all of the official designations under the City God, but could infer some clues from the titles called out by the Yin emissaries.

The visitors did not pay much attention to Ji Yuan, who was still sitting in the courtyard, and focused all their attention on the well.

“Sure enough, the suffocating aura here has suddenly diminished. I wonder what happened?”

“I heard what Lord Cheng Huang3 said, that this evil ghost has been roaring fiercely tonight. Although I don’t know why, I suspect that it has been severely injured!”

The figure titled Wu Pan turned his head to look at Ji Yuan at front of the house, making the latter a little nervous.

“This person is the new mortal who moved to Ju’an Pavilion? Is there anything unusual?”

The emissary who had previously been posted in the courtyard immediately replied.

“Lord Wu Pan, this person should be a mortal who couldn’t sleep due to the sudden attack of hostile intent. There is nothing abnormal.”

“Hm!”

During the brief exchange between the four officials of the City God, who were clearly high ranking based on their appearance and clothing, several Yin agents patrolled the inside and outside of the courtyard, presumably investigating something.

Not long after, a number of underworld agents came to report.

“Reporting to the Lords, there is nothing abnormal near Tian Niu Square!”

Several officials of the City God glanced at each other and thought for a moment.

“Could it be that some expert passed by this placed and helped us in Ning’an County?”

“Don’t think too much about it. Wait until we subdue this beast first, and then we can conduct a detailed investigation!”

“Time waits not for us, I’m afraid more urgent things might change!”

“That’s right!”

Judge Wu waved his sleeves and a black judge’s brush appeared in his hand. His eyes swept across the courtyard.

“The officers of each department will prepare the Soul Locking Array. The soul-binding envoys4 will wait for my order, ready your soul-binding chains!”

“We are under your command!”

“We are under your command!”

…

The four officials of the City God moved to the four corners of the courtyard. One stood with his sleeves down, the other took out a brush, one held a book in his hands, and the last carried a steel whip.

‘It’s coming, it’s coming! They’re about to take action! But what is it in the well that requires such a big fight? And don’t any of them want to persuade the mortal audience to leave?’

Ji Yuan was half nervous and half expectant, not knowing what would happen.

It wasn’t until several Yin agents holding black command flags turned into mist and disappeared that Ji Yuan realized that the yard in front of him was covered with a thin layer of ink. It seemed to be a kind of soul-chaining array, reaching just out to the edge of the eaves where he sat.

Inside the Soul Locking Formation, Judge Wu watched all the preparations and snorted coldly in the direction of the well.

“Are you still holding your breath, or maybe you’re afraid to show up because you’re hurt? You will die tonight! Soul-Binding Emissaries, take action!”

Judge Wu gave the order, and nine black-robed Yin agents holding long staffs surrounding the well. The Yin agents suddenly touched their waists in unison, and a dim light illuminated their belts, turning them into dark chains.

“Go!”

The nine soul-binding messengers roared together, and threw their soul-binding chains towards the mouth of the well like lightning, watching them sink into the ground around it.

Woo… woo… woo woo…

In an instant, an ominous wind picked up, and the old jujube tree’s branches and leaves in the courtyard swayed. Even outside the array, Ji Yuan still felt the chill surrounding him, and the goosebumps that ran up and down on his skin were as large as little beans!

“Aaaaaaahhh- Uuuuh-“

“Hmph, you can only scream. Everyone, help the soul-binders pull it up!”

As he spoke, the four officials of the City God boldly took action. They stretched their left hands that held their magic weapons forward, one after the other, and the yin qi grew in the courtyard, surrounding the soul binders.

For a moment, the soul-binding chains glowed brightly!

“Rise!”

“Aaaaahhhhh-“

The thick and densely matted hair sprang out of the well and thrashed wildly in the courtyard. Countless hairs immediately whipped around nine Yin emissaries, who leaped outward in an instant. A large section of the soul-binding chains were taken out.

“Cut!”

The Yin agents who had been waiting for the order pulled out their knives and chopped the hair chasing the soul-binders.

“Oooooooooohhhhh-“

The ghost’s shrill, howling voice grew louder and louder, and a large mass of dirty hair was yanked out of the well by the soul-binding chains, twisting and writhing in mid-air.

Ji Yuan’s pupils contracted violently, and his teeth chattered. Was that the thing that had attacked him just now?

The flesh and blood of the ghost trapped in the soul-binding chain seemed to be constantly churning, its bloodshot eyes and pale face also changing with each moment, and its yin qi was constantly pouring out.

The strong sense of fear stimulated from the visual and soul-impacting instinct was suffocating. It was simply incomparable to any reproduction in a horror movie.

“Evil! Tonight, you die!”

Judge Wu roared angrily, the judge’s brush pointed forward, and the other three officials of the City God also attacked.

Boom! Boom! Boom! Boom!

In the courtyard of Ju’an Pavilion, there seemed to be an explosion of yin energy.

T/n: I didn’t mention it before, but if you’re interested, feel free to read more about City/Land/State Gods in Ancient China

，纠察大人: Technically speaking, my translation is like saying their titles twice, since their titles are also their names. However, they are referred to by different titles later on that draw from characters in the pinyin translation of their original characters (i.e. the “wǔ pàn dàren,” or “Lord of Martial Judgement,” becomes Lord Wu or Judge Wu), so to prevent confusion when I switch between them I am repeating them several times here. Just keep in mind that they’re being called by a title. (In other news, if anyone knows the history/significance of these figures from our-world Ancient China please let me know! I’m interested but couldn’t find much through my research)

Another way of saying Lord City God, I will probably be using them interchangeably, at least until we get a different name for him (if we ever do).

The rawsand MTL literally translate it to “soul-seducer,” but the character in question, 勾 (gōu), has a couple other meanings (few of which particularly fit) so I used one that I thought worked best.

Chapter 28: Too Exciting

The strong yin qi already made Ji Yuan feel uncomfortable, but he also found that there were at least two Yin emissaries always in position in front of him. They were most likely intentionally blocking a considerable part of the yin qi impact for him, as he was a mortal.

At this moment in the courtyard, the four major officials under the City Gods were showing off their power, and most of the other Yin emissaries were on standby, except for the nine soul-binding messengers who were controlling the the soul-binding lock.

He had described it as a battle, but in Ji Yuan’s view, the terrifying ghost had been bound in the air by a soul-binding lock, and they had beaten him, well, literally.1

When the judge’s brush hit the ghost with a whipping motion or some other attack, it let out a piercing scream, and some of the evil energy was reduced.

This was not the first time that doubts and theories had arisen in Ji Yuan’s mind regarding his eyesight, but this time his hunch had only deepened. Not only could he see all kinds of evil spirits and ghosts, but he could even see the waves of evil energy being dispersed.

It seemed that, realizing it was at a critical moment, the ghost locked in the Soul Binding Chain began to struggle even more violently.

“Aaaaaahhhh-“

Amidst its screams, countless pale arms rushed out, grabbing at the surrounding Yin emissaries.

The City God’s subordinate officials waves their magic weapons, and the courtyard was once more filled with unnatural yin qi. They immediately blocked most of the Yin emissaries from the ghost’s clawing arms, but six or seven were still caught, including three of the soul-binding messengers.

“Clang! Clang!”

An agent tried to slash at the ghost’s claws with a knife to help his companions escape, but the sword made a sound like gold and iron clashing as it hit.

“Hooooo uuuuh….”

“Aaaaahhh!!!”

“Aaah!!!”

The seven Yin agents held by the ghost were directly smashed to pieces in its pale arms, turning into a cloud of black mist that was rapidly sucked into the claws. Three of the soul-binding chains lost control, and they were like three big whips in the struggle of the ghosts.

“Bang! Bang! Bang!”

A large number of Yin agents were directly hit by the soul-binding chains. They were stunned for a brief moment and flung into the air away from each other.

“How bold!!!”

The four major officials were stunned and jumped forward together. The four yin energies imbued with the scent of sandalwood swirled together in the sky above the small courtyard, and intertwined into a large net.

“Go!!!”

The big net fell from the sky, covering the ferocious ghost who was almost about to escape.

Whooooo… woooooo…

The powerful gusts of wind in the courtyard were no longer just induced by yin energies, but were now a true gale. The branches of the jujube tree swayed violently, and the fallen leaves and other objects in the courtyard seemed to be flying around randomly, without being restricted by the formation. Ji Yuan could do nothing but reach his hands out in fear in front of his face to block the dust and leaves.

At this moment, Ji Yuan was not only shocked, but also terrified.

‘Damn, this is what I dealt with just now? Fortunately, this thing likes to play with its food! If it was like this at the beginning, I would have been killed before even getting up!’

Judge Wu spun the brush in his hand and roared loudly.

“Draw out its soul energy!!!”

The other three officials of the City God also launched their magic weapons into the attack. The Yin emissaries followed and took action one after the other, attacking through the big net, particularly using their soul-binding chains. Every time they hit, the ghost trembled.

“Whoooooo-“

The scream almost pierced Ji Yuan’s eardrums, and the ghost in the giant net swelled rapidly. Lord Wu noticed that something was wrong and withdrew his brush, sharpened the tip of the bristles like a needle, and slammed it towards the middle of the net.

“Break it!”

“Bang!!”

Yin qi exploded, all directed towards Wu Pan.

With a bang, Wu Pan was blown away by the yin qi, and a gap instantly appeared in the giant net restraining the terrifying ghost.

“Not good!!!”

“Block it!!!”

The other three officials of the City God immediately combined their strength, but they couldn’t make up for the gap left by the Lord of Martial Judgement, and the ghost was already about to escape.

With the formation layout of Ju’an Pavilion, this ominous creature might not be able to escape to harm Ning’an County as a whole, but the underworld forces in the Soul Locking Formation were certainly afraid that they would suffer.

Ji Yuan didn’t know just how critical the situation was, but even as a normal layman he would be truly blind if he couldn’t tell that it was very dangerous.

Sitting in the yard at this time seemed a little unsafe…

Out of fear, Ji Yuan subconsciously stood up, intending to run away, but something unexpected happened.

As Ji Yuan rose from his seat, the increasingly violent struggles of the evil ghost in the courtyard suddenly abated, and he shrank into the soul-binding net as if severely frightened.

“This is a good opportunity! Don’t hesitate!”

At that roar, the Lord of Martial Judgement returned to his position.

A mere ghost would not make fools of the officials and Yin agents of the City God. The air behind the four chief officials under the jurisdiction of the City God began to fill with shadows and the scent of incense. Their figures seemed to grow, and their official robes swelled.

The giant net shone with gray light and squeezed. The four chief officials waved their hands, and four soul-binding chains flew forward. As they were swung, the chains seemed to turn into four spiritual snakes, and tightly wrapped around each other, emitting gray light.

After the thrilling experience just now, they understood that, even if this ferocious ghost was severely damaged, it could not easily be taken down without paying a heavy price. At this moment, they readily exhausted all their power!

“Mysterious City God, sharing your divine light with the earthly realm, expel this evil and bind the spirit, illuminate and observe the eight corners of the land!”2

From the direction of the City God Temple, the power like incense-smoke flew into the courtyard of the Ju’an Pavilion, augmenting the chief officer’s power.

“Although our Ning’an County has no more than 10,000 people under its jurisdiction, I will not allow you, you evil creature, to cause chaos and death!!!”

The judge’s brush, the soul whip, the book of laws, and the robes of blessing and longevity used all their strength to attack the evil ghost at this perfect moment.

“Boom…”

It sounded like thunder rumbling in the courtyard of Ju’an Pavilion.

. . .

Ji Yuan had been holding his breath for a while. In fact, if he had a stopwatch, he would definitely be surprised had just how long he was able to do so.

It wasn’t until the shrill screams in the courtyard became weaker and weaker and the ghost finally turned to ashes that, at last, Ji Yuan let out a sigh of relief.

‘Fortunately, the righteous side won! ’

Ji Yuan, who had gone weak in the knees, sat back on the small stool at the front of the house. But as soon as he regained his breath, he felt something was wrong.

He looked up and saw, to his surprise, that the energy of the formation in the courtyard had disappeared… And all of the chief officials of the four divisions under the City God stood in the courtyard facing him.

Ji Yuan’s throat twitched slightly, but he didn’t dare to swallow his saliva. They weren’t going to kill him, were they?

After a long time, the Lord of Martial Judgement took the lead, and the other three chief officials and various emissaries stepped forward one after another. They saluted with both hands.

“We were blind, and did not recognize this master’s identity!”

“Thank you for your help!”

Everyone thanked him in unison.

Before, they had wondered whether there had been an expert crossing the county border who had come down and injured the evil spirit, but they really hadn’t expected that same expert to have been sitting leisurely in the courtyard the whole time. Truly, real masters were those who didn’t reveal themselves, while those who revealed themselves weren’t true masters!

The chief officer of the Fourth Division did not say much. Judging from the previous actions of this man in the courtyard, he naturally didn’t want to be disturbed, so after repeatedly bowing, he led the various Yin emissaries under the City God’s jurisdiction out of Ju’an Pavilion. He would report to the City God first, then make a decision later.

Ji Yuan also didn’t know what to say to them. He really didn’t know much about things like the City God. After thinking about it for a while, he didn’t have any clue on how to proceed, so he simply didn’t say anything.

To be honest, in this situation, he didn’t want to act as a mastermind, but he really had been the one to seriously injure the ghost. Since he wasn’t lying, he should accept their words of thanks as a matter of course.

When the small courtyard returned to tranquility, and only a gentle breeze remained, Ji Yuan collapsed onto the stool and slumped like a dead dog, rubbing his heart, which had been repeatedly stimulated over the course of the night.

He raised his head and opened his tired eyes to look at the well in the courtyard. The eerie feeling had been swept away, and he couldn’t help but feel more at ease.

‘If things like this keep happening, I’m going to get a heart attack one day!’

After some time calming himself down, Ji Yuan had enough energy to think.

For example, what was that thing that was originally in the well? No matter how ignorant he was, he could tell that it definitely wasn’t an ordinary ghost. Or, for example, why hadn’t the Ning’an County’s City God come in person, despite the temple being so close by? Perhaps there was another reason.

Play on “to beat” as in “to defeat,” and “to beat” as in “to repeatedly injure”

Chant to activate the power on behalf of/given to them by the City God. Think of it like a D&D Paladin class invoking their deity. Here’s a breakdown: 幽幽 (yōuyōu): faint/indistinct, translated to “mysterious”; 城隍: Cheng Huang/city god; 和 (hé): harmonious/together with; 尘 (chén): dust/dirt/earth; 同 (tóng or tòng): together/same/see; 光 (guāng): light; 驱邪 (qūxié): drive out evils/exorcise; 缚 (fù): bind; 魅 (mèi): demon; 照 (zhào): illuminate/in accordance with; 见 (jiàn or xiàn): to see/to appear/to meet; 八 (bā): eight; 荒 (huāng): desolate, indicates eight corners of the land

Chapter 29: Xiao Yin Qing

[T/n note on title]1

Since he was physically and mentally exhausted, Ji Yuan didn’t think about it any more, and instead chose to go back to his room to sleep.

He had previously been unable to sleep for most of the night, and was also greatly frightened. Now, he made his way to the bed and feel asleep in no time at all.

This time, he slept until the sun rose at dawn.

“Hooo…”

Ji Yuan yawned and stretched. He got up from the bed and looked in the direction of the door and window. Even with the window paper covering the view, he could tell that it was already bright outside.

After getting dressed, he moved to open the door. The slanting sunlight shone on his body, making Ji Yuan feel warm.

He was full of energy, and there was no pain or itching in his body. It seemed that there would be no sequelae from the previous night. Ji Yuan planned to try his special vital energy abilities later to see if they had been affected.

In the courtyard, the jujube branches swayed slightly, and the sunlight shone through the leaves, casting mottles patches of light and shadow on the ground. The gloomy feel of Ju’an Pavilion had disappeared.

As someone with the soul of a 21st century youth, what was the first thing to do upon waking up? Of course, it was brushing his teeth and washing himself!2

In the past, in Yunlai Inn, the inn workers would bring fresh willow branches3 and water every day, but now he had to do it himself.

Looking at the well in the courtyard, and remembering the vision of the ghost emerging from it last night, Ji Yuan took less than a fraction of a second to reject the idea of using that well water.

‘In the future, let’s use the water from outside…’

Ji Yuan felt that, even if he wasn’t a germophobe, none of the friends in his previous life would use this well either.

After going out into the yard, Ji Yuan directly picked up the wooden board to cover the well, and then pressed the stones back on top of it.

He looked at the big empty vat and the two buckets outside the kitchen door. Well, it looked like he would have to fetch some water.

He had never seen pork or pigs running,4 and he had never carried water himself, but how could this possibly be more difficult than coming up with a plan to evade the tiger spirit, or facing the evil ghost head-on?

. . .

Ten minutes later, by Tian Niu Square’s public water wells,5 two large wells with windlasses6 could be seen, surrounded by a lot of slabs of bluestone and ditches to divert the water.

From all the way at his house, Ji Yuan could hear a lot of people talking about how they had heard a terrifying scream last night. Some people were also saying that there was a lot of movement from the direction of Ju’an Pavilion the other day, and that they suspected that someone new had moved in.

‘Based on this information, I can conclude that the loud noises in the second half of the night were blocked by the formation. Otherwise, these people wouldn’t be so calm. But I could hear it all last night, despite being outside the formation; it seems that my hearing is not just limited to the normal range!’

Ji Yuan, a handsome stranger in a wide-sleeved robe, came here to fetch water, which naturally attracted a lot of attention in Tian Niu Square, particularly from the girls and women who were doing laundry.

Despite hearing their whispers and feeling their gazes, Ji Yuan was not embarrassed at all. On the contrary, he was quite happy. In his previous life, his appearance didn’t turn nearly as many heads.

Thick-skinned people are more likely to be happy!

“Hey, who is this…”

“I’ve never seen him before, he looks so good!”

“He came here to fetch water, so he must live in Tian Niu Square or nearby, right?”

“I don’t know!”

The sound of chatter was mixed with the sound of rubbing and washing clothes, along with the clap of wooden boards. There were also others chatting about family issues, complaining about parents, and the footsteps of people passing by.

Ji Yuan rolled up his sleeves and tied the cuffs at his elbows. He used a the windlass reel to fetch some fresh water. It was a very novel experience for him. The bucket attached to the reel was small, and it took two repetitions to fill one of Ji Yuan’s buckets. Soon enough, after four trips, both buckets were full.

During this process, Ji Yuan also patiently observed the people of Tian Niu Square. Hearing their stories and filial piety, it seemed that this kind of atmosphere in ancient times was much richer than the community in the previous life.

Well, people were also a lot more shy. Whenever Ji Yuan turned his head in search of a sound, the younger girls would look away and dared not glance back.

Ji Yuan filled his buckets, put the pole in place,7 picked up the load, and splendidly took a step forward.

“Crash!”

The water in the bucket at the back spilled directly onto his clothes. Ji Yuan subconsciously wanted to avoid it, but as a result the two buckets swayed even more violently. His back heel hit the bucket, causing them to go flying, water spilling everywhere.

At this moment, his extremely bad eyesight only made things worse. His whole body swayed back and forth like he was dancing Yangko.8

“Hey, hey…”

Steady, steady!

“Bang! Bang!”

Both buckets fell off, and half of Ji Yuan’s clothes were splashed with water.

“Ahahahahaha…”

“Hehehehe…”

“Uhahahahahaha…”

The women and passersby burst into laughter when they saw this scene.

“He really is such a gentleman!”

“Hahahaha, Mom, he can’t even pick up water!”

“Ouch, I’m dying of laughter, I’ve never seen anyone carry water like this and not immediately sway to death, hahahahaha…”

Damn, damn, dammit! What a disappointment!

It turns out that carrying water is also a skill!

Even though he had honed his thick skin for two lifetimes, his face still felt hot. And in front of everyone, too, his appearance really took a dive!

Fortunately, there are no acquaintances around!

However, the way to get rid of this embarrassment was actually very simple. As long as he didn’t feel embarrassed, it was fine. In any case, he could blame it on his eyes not working well, so why would he bother saving face?

So Ji Yuan also shook his head and laughed casually.

“Hahahaha… It’s not easy to carry water!”

After saying this with a smile, Ji Yuan leaned over and stretched out his hand to fumble around for the bucket and the pole that were lying on the ground. This action was very different from expected, and finally made the people around him notice Ji Yuan’s eyes.

“Hey, that person’s eyesight doesn’t seem to be very goo!”

“Yeah, just now I was wondering why he couldn’t seem to open his eyes…”

“This… he still comes to fetch water by himself?”

…

The laughter was gone. It seemed that most people in Ning’an County were still simple and wouldn’t really laugh at a blind man. Well, except for that damn broker.

“Sir, let me help you carry the water!”

A clear voice sounded from the side. Ji Yuan heard footsteps approaching. At this moment, he had just touched the pole, and he looked up and around to pinpoint the sound. It was a boy who looked to be around eleven or twelve years old, with tanned skin and handsome features.

Huh? Handsome features? I can actually see him clearly!

Ji Yuan couldn’t help but take a closer look at the little boy who took the initiative to help. According to his the experience in the past two days, it seemed that anyone who he could see clearly was not ordinary.

“What’s your name, little kid? How old are you?”

The little boy was a bit stunned when he saw Ji Yuan’s pale eyes, and it took him a while to react.

“My name is Yin Qing, I’m 12 years old. I often help my family carry water. I’m very strong!”

Ji Yuan nodded with a smile, and then listened to the surrounding voices. He couldn’t hear any of the child’s family was nearby, but there was a group of children playing around not too far away. It seemed that Yin Qing had come out to play with them by himself.

“Thank you then!”

It was really embarrassing to let a child carry water, but Ji Yuan also wanted to use this time to observe Yin Qing.

“It’s alright, it’s alright!”

As soon as Ji Yuan finished speaking, Yin Qing had already picked up two buckets, taken the pole, and run back to the well. He looked like a lively, cheerful, and helpful ordinary child.

. . .

“Sir,9 where is your home? Have we arrived yet? Do you still want to keep going?”

He and Ji Yuan walked deeper and deeper together towards the east corner of Tian Niu Lane,10 and there were fewer and fewer people around.

“It’s almost there, right in front!”

Ji Yuan looked at this child from time to time, and felt that he gave off a very spiritual feeling.

After a while, Yin Qing stopped outside the courtyard of Ju’an Pavilion, glancing at Ji Yuan, who had also stopped, with a sad face.

“Sir… you live here…”

He was looking at Ji Yuan as if he had seen something odd.

“Yes, I live here. I just moved in yesterday. Why, wasn’t Yin Xiaowa’er11 going to help me carry the water in?”

I may have already mentioned this, but “Xiao” is a casual honorific that means “little,” usually used for children.

T/n: … okay, be honest here…

Teeth-cleaning twigs, same concept as modern-day dog “toothbrushes” – you chew it until the end is frayed, and the motion makes it brush against the teeth.

Think of it like saying, “I’ve never lived in a rural area like this”

No exact translation, but it seems to break down to that meaning: 双 (shuāng): two/double/pair; 井 (jǐng): a well; 浦 (pǔ): shore/drainage ditch. Water wells in squares were common in each area of the neighborhood.

A large winch for moving heavy weights. It’s a classic sight on top of wells to reel the bucket back up.

Carrying pole

Chinese folk dance

大先生 respectful and formal way to address someone, like “Mr.” but even more respect: “great sir,” “grand gentleman,” maybe “Mr. Sir”

Full disclosure: the translation is technically Tian Niu Lane, but the context seems to be used interchangeably with “Square.” I’ve translated it as “Square” in the past, and will probably stick to that.

Xiaowa – child, er – suffix that means little, so he’s calling him “little child Yin”

Chapter 30: The Lord City God Invites You

Yin Qing hesitated when he arrived.

“Mr. Ji, this place… My father and mother won’t let us come near this place…”

Xiao Yin Qing didn’t know how to explain it to Ji Yuan. If he said it bluntly, this honest-looking gentleman might misunderstand him.

Ji Yuan saw his troubled look, and didn’t plan on making things difficult for the child. Although he knew that the evil creature in this house had been exorcised by the four officials under the City God, the other people in town didn’t know.

“Okay, just put the bucket and pole by the door. I’ll bring them inside myself.”

Ji Yuan said, smiling as he took the initiative to help Xiao Yin Qing unload the pole. The child had clearly helped Ji Yuan carry the water a long way, and his small face was a little troubled.

“Sir, you… please don’t stay here! Here…”

Yin Qing swallowed and looked at the sun in the sky, still a little afraid to stand at the gate of the courtyard and speak the thoughts in his heart.

The elders in the family had told him not to talk about the Ju’an Pavilion, especially to its residents. It was too taboo, and his life would be over if he caught the attention and resentment of something evil.

Seeing that he wanted to say something but didn’t dare, Ji Yuan understood somewhat and patted Yin Qing’s head.

“Call me Mr. Ji. It’s rare to see such warmheartedness at such a young age. As for this Ju’an Pavilion, you don’t have to worry. I’ll be fine living here! Go on back, don’t let your family worry.”

“Then, Mr. Ji, I’ll be leaving now…”

Yin Qing didn’t dare stand at the entrance of Ju’an Pavilion for much longer. It was already pushing it to come all the way there. After saying goodbye, he quickly trotted away.

At that moment, watching Yin Qing leave, Ji Yuan suddenly felt something inexplicable, and turned to look at the other side of the street.

Not very far away stood a man who looked like an official. He wore a white robe and a tall cap.1 His footsteps gave Ji Yuan the sense that it was an illusion, particularly as there was no sound.

A ghost? Could some ghosts really come out during the day?

The servant suddenly moved next to Ji Yuan and bowed to him.

“Daytime patrol Zuo Guan, under the jurisdiction of Ning’an County’s City God, greets Mr. Ji!”

Ji Yuan wasn’t too afraid. Seeing the official’s appearance, he guessed that it was about the evil of Ning’an County’s City God officials had expelled the night before. That short experience had also given him a good impression of the City God of Ning’an County.

As for why this official knew that his surname was Ji, perhaps he heard it just now, or perhaps they had some means of finding out.

“What can I do for you?”

Since the official had spoken, Ji Yuan had to reply.

Not too far away, Yin Qing, who had not yet run off, heard Ji Yuan’s voice behind him, and subconsciously stopped and looked back.

‘Is this Sir talking to me? ’

When he turned around, he only saw Ji Yuan facing in the other direction of the courtyard gate, as if he were talking to someone, but on the path no one was there.

Shivering, Yin Qing fled.

At the same time, the daytime patrol official respectfully answered Ji Yuan’s question.

“The Lord City God has invited Mr. Ji to the Temple of the City God to chat. If it is convenient for Mr. Ji, I can lead you there now.”

Now? Going to see Ning’an County’s City God?

Ji Yuan, who was calm on the surface, had an apprehensive feeling in his heart that he was about to meet the powerful leaders of the city. As for whether or not to go, there was no need to deliberate over his choice.

“Please wait a moment while I carry these two buckets of water inside.”

“Of course, Mr. Ji!”

Ji Yuan didn’t say anything more. He picked up the pole, hooked the buckets with the rope hook, and stood up very carefully. He did his best to imitate Yin Qing’s example, holding the rope with both hands positioned at the front and back, instead of grabbing the pole.

At last, he managed not to lose face in front of the daytime patrol officer, and he safely carried the water to the door of the kitchen in the courtyard.

The daytime patrol officer waited right outside the courtyard, watching Ji Yuan struggle to lift the bucket and pour water into the vat. A lot of water even splashed on Ji Yuan’s clothes. He didn’t look like a mysterious expert at all.

It was just that, he didn’t know why, but the clumsier he seemed, the more respectful the daytime patrol officer was, and by the end of it he didn’t even dare to stand too casually.

Lord Chenghuang had said before that the so-called true cultivators of the Immortal Palace who traveled around from place to place were arrogant and imposing. They were more like villains than gentlemen, and most of them could not be considered true experts. A true master followed the natural way of heaven, and returned to the natural state!2

“Haah…”

Ji Yuan put down the bucket and breathed a sigh of relief. He looked at the corners of his sleeves and clothes. They were a little wet.

However, he had already figured it out before falling asleep. He had no intention of pretending to be an expert in front of the City God, so he acted casually without any pressure.

After rolling down the cuffs of his sleeves and casually brushing himself down, Ji Yuan walked out of the courtyard gate, and closed the door before looking at the daytime patrol officer.

“Let’s go, please lead the way. This is my first time in Ning’an County, so I don’t know where the Temple of the City God is!”

Ji Yuan didn’t care too much about brushing his teeth anymore, he simply decided to go.

“Very well! Mr. Ji, please follow me!”

The daytime patrol official stretched out his hand in invitation, but instead of letting him lead as Ji Yuan had feared, he walked forward.

After Ji Yuan caught up with him, he slowed down, as if he were walking by Ji Yuan’s side. Ji Yuan glanced at him but didn’t know what to say, so he just continued to walk forward. At worst, he could just ask if he didn’t recognize who he was supposed to be meeting.

After walking a hundred paces, they reached the first fork in the road. The daytime patrol official strode quickly to the left, and and stretched out his hand again.

“Mr. Ji, this way please!”

“Uh, okay!”

When Ji Yuan walked past, the daytime patrol officer was accompanying him once more.

‘This is so… a bit embarrassing…I’m really not a big-shot…’

The attitude of the daytime patrol officer was too respectful, and Ji Yuan felt extremely awkward. Even Lu Chengfeng didn’t act like this on that day.

In a small alley of Tian Niu Square, a daytime patrol officer invisible to ordinary people slowly proceeded, accompanied by the elegant and graceful Ji Yuan.

In order to alleviate the embarrassment, Ji Yuan could only try to find topics to chat with the ghostly patrol official about.

“I wonder what my guide’s surname is?”

Although the ghost didn’t need to breathe, Ji Yuan could feel that the daytime patrol official seemed to be relieved.

“I don’t dare accept your recognition.3 My surname was Liu before I was reborn, and my single name is Jiang!”

“Oh, Daytime Officer Liu!”4

“I don’t dare!”

Ji Yuan felt that this manner of speaking was a bit annoying, but when in Rome, do as the Romans do.5

“Daytime Officer Liu was a native of Ning’an before his death, right?”

“That’s right. Before my death, I was a native of Xiaxiaowanhe6 Village in Ning’an County. I used to work in the Ning’an County Government Office. Because I was kind and conscientious in life, when I died I was promoted to the daytime patrol by the county’s City God. It has been twenty two years since then!”

Ji Yuan could see that under the had of the daytime patrol officer, his face looked like that of a middle-aged man, and his heart was no longer quite so calm.

‘Damn, I’m afraid that this daytime patrol officer is older than my grandfather…’

“Daytime Officer Liu brought peace and eliminated violence in life, and protected the county even after his death. You were a good man in both Yin and Yang,7 which is admirable!”

Although his words were deliberately flattering, most of them were sincere.

Civil servants such as Daytime Officer Liu should be respected no matter where he was, let alone in ancient times.

“Mr. Ji flatters me, he flatters me very much!”

He said, but Ji Yuan could see that the daytime patrol officer was still very happy at his words.

A compliment and affirmation wouldn’t bring him much benefit, but it could make good people – rather, good ghosts – happy.

The atmosphere was soon rid of the previous embarrassment and became harmonious. Ji Yuan was walking, and suddenly his heart lurched. He glanced at the front corner of the alley, smiled, and continued to talk with the daytime patrol officer while walking.

Further ahead, Xiao Yin Qing’s heart was thumping, and his feet ran away without stopping.

It was too scary, it was too scary, that gentleman was walking alone but seemed to be chatting with someone. He was probably talking with a ghost!

The ghost in the small Ju’an’s Pavilion followed this gentleman out!

Most likely a Futou

You might have seen this translated in other stories as “back to basics” or “return to nature.” It’s the concept that, once you get far enough in your cultivation, you have opened yourself up to the world so much that you and your energy have become indistinguishable from it – similar to some Buddhist denominations’ idea of enlightenment.

不敢称贵 Lit: “I don’t dare accept your praise” but it’s more like recognition. LR is basically saying “oh my gosh this big-shot cares about the name of a puny ghost like me,” hence the excessive “don’t dare”s

日巡 Rixun, title for the underworld daytime patrol. It’s not a proper honorific, so I’m translating it.

A Western saying this time: adapt yourself to the customs of the place you are in.

小 (xiǎo): small; 湾 (wān): bay/gulf; 河 (hé): river, roughly translates to Small River Gulf Village, I’ll be leaving it untranslated since it’s not too important of a place

Death and life

Chapter 31: Old City God

Xiao Yin Qing’s curiosity prompted the child, who thought he was well-hidden, to stare at Ji Yuan secretly for a while. However, the more he looked, the more unsettled he became, and finally he couldn’t help but run away.

Yin Qing was frightened and didn’t dare to go back to find his friends who he came out to play with. What did children do when they were afraid? They ran home to find their parents!

So Yin Qing did exactly that and ran all the way home, out of breath.

Yin Qing’s family also lived in Tian Niu Square. Even if it was at an angle and a couple turns away from Ju’an Pavilion, the straight-line distance was actually only a few hundred meters.

The Yin family’s house had a small courtyard with low walls, a building with the front hall, and another with the back rooms. Only a simple screen separated the fairly spacious front hall into different spaces. There was a section for the living room, a place for the Yin family’s father and son to study, and a path that connected to the kitchen. The outside of the main house gave the impression of an average, middle-to-upper-class family.

Yin Qing ran home in one breath, pushed open the courtyard door with a bang, and rushed into the hall, startling the Yin family’s mother1 who was in the middle of weaving at home.

“Mom! Mom! Um, over there, there was a gentleman, he, haah…haah… Then that gentleman and the ghost, haah…haah…”

“Don’t fret, speak clearly, why is that Sir not a gentleman?”

Mother Yin took out a handkerchief to wipe Yin Qing’s sweat.

“How old are you, look at how incoherent your speech is!”

A stern voice came, startling Yin Qing, and even suppressing the fear of having potentially witnessed a ghost.

“Dad, you’re home…”

Yin Qing turned his head and noticed his father sitting on a chair by the window in the hall, reading from an open book.

“Qing’er, your father was recommended by Mr. Zhu and Mr. Zhou to be the head teacher of the new school in the county. From now on, he no longer needs to go to Zhu’s house to be a private tutor.”

“Head teacher? Really, Dad? When will you start?”

Yin Qing was very excited when he heard the news.

“Haha, of course it’s true. It will take a few more days, but it won’t be too long!”

Father Yin replied, stroking his beard contentedly.

“When the time comes, we can go to the school together. Don’t mess around outside all day. Study the classics and the works of the sages, gain fame, and achieve success in the imperial examinations. That is the proper path forward!”

“Oh……”

Yin Qing actually hated studying, but he didn’t dare refute his father.

In truth, there were many places to study in Ning’an County, but most of them were private schools run by old scholars, none of whom had outstanding academic qualifications.

This school could be regarded as a relatively formal, high-end place of learning in Ning’an County. In theory, it was only lower in status than the formal academies. Moreover, this school was geared towards a younger age group, while the academy aimed for a higher age group. Many families would choose to let their children study when they were young, and continue to the academy when they grew older.

Yin Zhaoxian, as an orthodox scholar who had obtained the second-place rank in the state examination, was definitely considered the best among the scholars in Ning’an County. He felt that it was a matter of course for him to be elected as the head of the school, and proud of his position.

“By the way, what were you so panicked about just now?”

Yin Zhaoxian put down the book in his hands and looked at Yin Qing.

“Oh, right, Dad, there is a new resident in that Ju’an Pavilion over there. He is an honest and kind gentleman, but he was talking to the ghost inside…”

“Shh!!!!”

Mother Yin immediately covered Yin Qing’s mouth.

“What is this nonsense?”

Yin Zhaoxian’s expression didn’t look very good. Even though he himself was quite knowledgeable, he knew much about areas that many in the village were otherwise ignorant of, he still avoided talking about Ju’an Pavilion. That place really was a bit too eerie.

Then Yin Zhaoxian suddenly realized something, and stared at his son and asked.

“How did you know?”

“Uh… This… That Sir’s eyesight is not good. Just now, he was fetching water in the public well square and the bucket was overturned. I just helped him carry the water back. Who knew he lived in that little Ju’an Pavilion…”

Yin Qing lowered his voice in apprehension.

“You went in?”

Mother Yin asked nervously. Althought it should be alright to enter Ju’an Pavilion on a sunny day, that please really was too evil. Yin Qing was a weak child, so the adults couldn’t help but feel nervous on his behalf.

“No, no, Dad and Mom have told me not to so many times, how would dare to go inside? I put down the load at the door. But after I ran away, I saw the gentleman talking in some direction right outside the courtyard. He carried the water into the yard and came back out, chatting while walking as if someone was following him. He also said something scary about life and death. I was so terrified that I ran straight home!”

After speaking, Yin Qing asked his father, fearful and curious.

“Dad, do you think the ghost in Ju’an Pavilion came out with that gentleman?”

Yin Zhaoxian also got goosebumps when he heard the story, and Mother Yin even covered Yin Qing’s mouth again.

“Okay, okay, don’t go there to play in the future. Also, don’t repeat any of this nonsense outside, understood?”

“Yeah, I got it!”

Mother Yin hugged Yin Qing and patted his head.

“Husband, why don’t you take Qing’er to the City God’s Temple to pay homage to Lord Chenghuang. Perhaps it will get rid of some bad luck?”

In the end, this was his own son, and Yin Zhaoxian would not wait around to find out if anything did go wrong. Some extreme scholars might have ridiculed the notion of supernatural powers, but the small Ju’an Pavilion truly was unsettling.

“Okay! After lunch, I’ll take Qing’er to the Temple of the City God to burn incense!”

A few years ago, when performing rituals, a trembling old Buddhist master2 mentioned that the City God of Ning’an County was suppressing him. Ever since then, the people of Tian Niu Square made sure to frequently worship the City God on New Year’s and during festivals.

. . .

Ji Yuan followed the daytime patrol officer through half of Ning’an County, all the way to the City God Temple on Temple Administration Lane. After having finally arrived in a more crowded place, Ji Yuan and the daytime patrol officer stopped chatting.

When they reached the bustling City God Temple, they saw an old man standing in front of an incense candle stall, and the daytime patrol officer hurriedly stepped forward to salute.

“Lord Chenghuang, Mr. Ji is here!”

Because there were pilgrims and people passing by, the Old City God just nodded to the officer.

The latter said, “I will take my leave!” and floated away on his own.

Ji Yuan originally thought that he would meet the City God in a relatively secluded place in the temple, but he didn’t expect that the latter would appear directly outside the temple in the form of a mortal old man.

He looked at the City God with some confusion,, but Ji Yuan’s half-open eyes made it impossible to tell whether his gaze was focused or scattered. He truly appeared like a calm, ancient well with no ripples disturbing the surface.

There was also a faint scent of sandalwood around the body of the City God, but it was much lighter than with the previous four chief officers.

At the same time, the City God was also observing Ji Yuan. His eyes appeared useless at first glance, but he seemed no different in his actions from ordinary people. He was blind, but there was no disruption; instead, he revealed a trace of faint, plain confusion. Not a mortal after all!

The mutual observation only lasted for a few seconds, and then the old man took the lead to break the silence.

“The Lord of Ning’an County, City God Song Shichang, thanks Mr. Ji for his great righteousness and aid in eradicating evil!”

When he saw the old man bowing over his hands, Ji Yuan didn’t dare be lulled into a false sense of security. Opposite him was a City God, a big-shot among spirits and gods, so he quickly joined in. He reminded himself that needed to be even more respectful than the old man.

“Lord City God, it’s quite embarrassing. I just used some humble tricks, and happened to help the chief officials of your city. It was nothing big!”

“Hahaha, Mr. Ji is being too modest. I know that Mr. Ji has not yet eaten. I have ordered a table of snacks in the building outside the temple. Let us make our way there and talk. After you!”

Ji Yuan also quickly imitated the City God and extended his hands for the invitation, not daring to refuse or make excuses.

“It is better to obey than be respectful, by all means!”3

Ji Yuan looked at the lively and bustling surroundings of the Temple of the City God. From time to time he could see people going in and out of the temple to worship the City God. With that very same City God next to him, he was more nervous now than when he arrived.

‘Relax, don’t stiffen your legs…’

If anyone saw the clay statues in the temple come to life and walk down to chat with them, they would probably understand Ji Yuan’s feeling at this moment.

MTL said “Auntie,” but I put it in an actual Chinese dictionary and it means “mom”: 阿 (ā): prefix before monosyllabic names, shows familiarity/kinship; 娘 (niáng): mother. Another win against MTL, muahahaha!

法师 (fǎshī): one who has mastered the sutras (Buddhism), prev. translated as “mage” since there’s no English equivalent. Thank you terf0x01

JY is saying “sure, I’ll go” instead of putting up a token protest for politeness’ sake

Chapter 32: The Whole Story

The outer building of the temple covered an area of ​​about half an acre, with three stories and a roof with eaves covered with glazed tiles. It was a famous dining and tea restaurant in Ning’an, and the food was also considered to be some of the best in the county.

Ji Yuan walked into the outer temple building with the City God of Ning’an County. The inside hosted ten tables, and nine of them were already full, so the room was very lively.

As soon as he entered the restaurant, the a waiter came up to them and enthusiastically asked.

“Two guests, please come in. There are private seats on the second and third floors. The second floor is lively and the third floor is quiet. Which floor would you prefer?”

The waiter had a discerning eye. Of these two men, the older man wore luxurious black robes, and his appearance was clean and tidy, with silver hair. Although the younger man next to him was dressed relatively simply in a wide-sleeved green robe, and his hairstyle was a bit messy at first glance, the more he looked, the more harmonious his appearance felt.

“If you would like, please take us up to the third floor. My surname is Song. Someone has already ordered refreshments for me.”

“Oh, oh, you are finally here. The shopkeeper has asked me three times today. Please come with me. The refreshments are ready!”

The waiter hurriedly invited the two of them upstairs, leading the way.

The Old1 City God and Ji Yuan smiled and followed.

In the seats on the first floor, everyone was chatting and joking, laughing happily. Overall it seemed very noisy.

As soon as Ji Yuan stepped onto the stairs, the sounds from the second floor became more pronounced.

“…speaking of those 9 knights, all of them were young, handsome, virtuous and extraordinary. Their only shortcoming was their young age; they were weak in experience but already strong in martial arts, including the heroine, who is certainly not inferior to the men… They soon uncovered the Ning’an County government bounty list, and made some preparations. Together, they climbed up Niu Kui Mountain, but that night, a storm came…”

“Oh…”

“How bold!”

“No!”

…

The voice of the storyteller on the second floor and the voices of exclamation from down below sounded one after another.

As Ji Yuan and the Old City God followed the waiter up the steps from the second to the third floor, they heard the storyteller begin to spin the tale of the tiger hunt.

“… Hero Du slashed at the tiger with his saber, and a flash of blood burst out of the slash wound. Hero Lu’s fists and palm strikes fell from the sky, and a strike that could break stone struck square on the white tiger’s head!”

“Oh!”

“It’s really exciting!”

….

Hearing these words, Ji Yuan, who was already halfway up the stairs to the third floor, couldn’t help but smile, and shook his head.

“It seems that Mr. Ji is interested in this storyteller’s tale?”

The Old City God asked him with a smile. In fact, he had also gone to see the white tiger skin along with other underworld agents. It was indeed majestic, but there were no knife wounds on it. Storytelling was the art of the common people,2 similar to the drama, and it was normal to embellish a story.

“Hahaha, I’m not very interested. I just happened to hear something that caught my attention.”

There was no way this story was true, those nine young heroes were so frightened in front of Lu Shanjun that they didn’t even dare move a muscle.3 It was such a strong contrast with this story.

The Old City God was thoughtful, but he didn’t ask any further questions.

The three of them arrived at the third floor together. It was obviously much quieter and less crowded. People were either drinking tea and chatting, or gazing at the scenery outside the building.

“Come on, our two distinguished guests, this is your table. We have bean paste cake, millet cake, fragrant fruit cake, rice porridge, pickled fruit, and candied fruit, as well as this pre-rain tea.4 Please help yourselves, and feel free to call if you need anything!”

The waiter said as he pointed out the various refreshments.

“Okay, thank you, little brother!”5

“Thank you!”

Ji Yuan and Old Chenghuang thanked him almost at the same time, then they both smiled and took seats opposite each other.

As the waiter walked off, he scratched his head and thought: these two guests were really polite!

After the waiter left, the City God turned back around and spoke.

“Mr. Ji, please try the tea from this outer temple building, the taste is quite good.”

After doing physical work early in the morning and walking such a long way, Ji Yuan was already quite hungry, so he didn’t hesitate to pick up the millet cake and take a bite.

The taste was not too heavy, it was crunchy as he bit into it, and there was an aroma of fresh rice and a slightly sweet taste.6

“It’s delicious! It’s really delicious! Lord City God should also try it!”

“As long as Mr. Ji likes it. I am a small City God in a remote county, and this form is just a clay sculpture that has turned into a spiritual incarnation, not a physical body.7 With a physical body, mortals can enjoy food to satisfy their cravings, so it’s a bit of a waste to only consume the energy.”

No physical body? Was this an avatar in front of him?

Ji Yuan didn’t know what else to say, so he could only continue eating by himself. Fortunately, drinking tea didn’t seem to have any effect on the other party.

After drinking a bowl of rice porridge and eating a few cakes, Ji Yuan stopped for a bit. The Old City God merely put down his tea up, and turned his attention away from the scenery outside the building.

“Mr. Ji helped our Ning’an County get rid of a serious problem this time…”

The Old Town God recalled and continued.

“Seven years ago, the Earth Dragon of Desheng Prefecture turned over8 and an evil spirit leaked out into the spiritual veins of the earth.9 Initially, it was nothing much to worry about; regardless of whether the sun continued to shine, or it thundered and rained, the evil spirit would naturally dissipate. Unfortunately, there was also a strong evil energy flowing along the underground water veins…”

The Old City God spoke, and then sighed.

“Underground water veins are inherently yin, and this evil spirit rushed out from a mass grave in the western part of the county. It was empowered by the yin energy of death and turned into this murderous creature. As the City God of this county, I was naturally aware of this. I personally led the subordinates of my various divisions to suppress it. Unexpectedly, this evil thing was very strange. I don’t know whether it had swallowed some Yin spirit, or if there was some other cause, but it actually gained awareness…”10

As the Old City God slowly explained, Ji Yuan gradually understood the cause and effect of these events.

The evil spirit born from the earth seemed to have been incredibly powerful. The ghost that had gained awareness and intelligence actually pretended to be ignorant to deceive the City God, and at a critical moment, it swallowed up the Chiefs of the Reporting Department and the Yin and Yang Department, and detonated its evil energy, seriously injuring the City God’s physical body.

Fortunately, although the Old City God suffered a big loss, old ginger was still spicy.11 When he was attacked by the earthly evil, he decisively consumed his incense essence and golden body and fought back fiercely, severely damaging the evil creature’s nascent enlightenment.

Although the evil spirit managed to escape after that battle, the Lord Cheng Huang sacrificed the Crown of the City God, sealing all of the evil energy inside of it. With the help of this tool, he found where the evil spirit was hiding: in the well of Ju’an Pavilion. He used all of his strength to lock the spirit’s Yin with his Yin, and temporarily sealed it in the depths of the well, so that the City God could recover from his injuries before finally eradicating it.

This tale made Ji Yuan break out in a cold sweat. Although the Old City God didn’t state it clearly, Ji Yuan’s understood that if this fierce evil thing were allowed to escape at that time, then Ning’an County would be in serious trouble. It might even have had a snowball effect, and an even greater disaster would have occurred.

As for the residents of Ju’an Pavilion, the accidents of the first two households were actually due to their natural fate, not the influence of evil spirits. By the time the third resident, the scholar, had moved in, the evil had most likely recovered, and was able to briefly escape to the soul-lock. The scholar, who was found not far from the well, was frightened to death.

Earthbound spirits like the City God also had their own constraints. Although they could protect one party, they could not directly affect the living world.12

Therefore, ever since then, the City God used dreams and other methods to spread rumors that it was unsafe to live in Ju’an Pavilion. As expected, it succeeded in preventing anyone from staying there, but he hadn’t expected that Ji Yuan would move in and come across the spirit again.

As this was a rare opportunity, the Old City God couldn’t wait any longer. He dispatched capable subordinates, and took up his post in the temple, mobilizing the accumulated incense in the temple. He put all of his efforts into fighting and finally destroyed the evil spirit. It was a big triumph for Ning’an County.

“Lao,” commonly-used honorific that means old, more on the casual side (which is kind of strange to use when addressing a God, but who am I to criticize JY’s willful ignorance?)

Lit: “Reciting stories is a doorway to the common people’s art.” I find this saying quite poetic.

Lit: “let out a fart”

Pre-rain tea refers to tea picked at the second harvest. The earlier tea is harvested, the higher the quality, since the leaves and the vitamins stored in them are fresh. The first harvest is pre-Ming or Ming Qian, which refers to a tea harvest that was picked before the traditional Qing Ming festival (April 4-6th) – pre-Ming is SUPER high quality, so much so that it’s usually reserved for the government to use as diplomatic gifts. Next is pre-rain, which is from Qing Ming to Gu Yu (aka the spring rains, April 19-20th), which is still very high quality but slightly less outrageously expensive than pre-Ming.

TL;DR: pretty high quality tea there

Not his actual little brother, but an honorific reference again
